6682               cv449
6683               	;
6684               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
6685               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
6686               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
6687               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
6688               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
6689               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
6690               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
6691               	; 	'
6692               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
6693               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
6694               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
6695               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
6696               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
6697               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
6698               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
6699               	; 	hacc = hacc + hsp4
6700 CCB6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HACC,r0
     CCB8 215D     
6701 CCBA B020  34 	ab @cvb_HSP4,r0
     CCBC 2194

     CCC0 215D

6988 CF22 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
6989 CF24 0450  20 	b *r0
6990               	;
6991               	; 	' ======================================================================
6992               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6993               	; 	' ======================================================================
6994               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6995               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6996               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6997               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6998               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6999               	; 	'
7000               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
7001               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
7002               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
7003               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
7004               	; 	' broken.
7005               	; 	'
7006               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
7007               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
7008               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
7009               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
7010               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
7011               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
7012               	; 	'
7013               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
7014               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
7015               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
7016               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
7017               	; coll_obst:
7018               cvb_COLL_OBST
7019               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
7020 CF26 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     CF28 2162

     CF2C 0300

7537               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
7538               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
7539               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
7540               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even

7565               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
7566               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7567               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7568               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7569               	; 	'
7570               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7571               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7572               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7573               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7574               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7575               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7576               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
7577               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

7701               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
7702               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
7703               	; 		'
7704               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
7705               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure

7707               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two

8316 DA62 0450  20 	b *r0
8317               	;
8318               	; 	' ======================================================================
8319               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8320               	; 	' ======================================================================
8321               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8322               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8323               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8324               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8325               	; 	'
8326               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8327               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8328               	; 	'
8329               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8330               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8331               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8332               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8333               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8334               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8335               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8336               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8337               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8338               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8339               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8340               	; esc_tick:
8341               cvb_ESC_TICK
8342               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8343               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8344               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8345               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8346               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8347 DA64 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     DA66 2151     
8348 DA68 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     DA6A 0100     
8349 DA6C 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8350 DA6E 1402  14 	jhe cv627
8351 DA70 0460  28 	b @cv626
     DA72 DA88     
8352               cv627
8353               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8354 DA74 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     DA76 2151     
8355 DA78 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     DA7A 0700     
8356 DA7C 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8357 DA7E 1A02  14 	jl cv629
8358 DA80 0460  28 	b @cv628
     DA82 DA88     
8359               cv629
8360 DA84 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8361 DA86 0450  20 	b *r0
8362               cv628
8363               	; 	END IF
8364               cv626
8365               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8366               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8367               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8368               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8369               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8370               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8371               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8372               	; 	'
8373               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8374               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8375               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8376               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8377               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8378               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8379               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8380               	; 	escp = escp + 1
8381 DA88 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     DA8A 20AF

     DA8E 0100

     DA92 20AF

8393               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
8394               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
8395               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
8396               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
8397               	; 	' 7 an east one.
8398               	; 	'
8399               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
8400               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
8401               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
8402               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
8403               	; 	'
8404               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
8405               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
8406               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
8407               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
8408               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
8409               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
8410               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
8411               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
8412               	; 	'
8413               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never

8671               	;
8672               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8673               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8674               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8675               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8676               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8677               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8678               	; 	'
8679               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8680               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8681               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8682               	; 	'
8683               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8684               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8685               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8686               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8687               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8688               	; 	'
8689               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8690               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8691               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8692               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8693               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8694               	; 	'
8695               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8696               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8697               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
8698               	; scan_furn:
8699               cvb_SCAN_FURN
8700               	; 	FOR fl = 0 TO 3

8916               	;
8917               	; 	' THE ELEVATOR MARKER TRACKS THE CAR, which is the whole point of it. It
8918               	; 	' used to be a static bar on all three shopping floors -- that says where
8919               	; 	' the shaft is, which never changes and which the player already knows,
8920               	; 	' and says nothing about the one fact that matters: whether the car is
8921               	; 	' where you are. Erase, move, redraw, exactly like an actor's dot.
8922               	; 	' ONE ROW NOW, AND WIDER FOR IT. The band is four pixel rows and each
8923               	; 	' one carries a single colour, so the car's grey row is the same row the
8924               	; 	' escalator heads use; spending a second row on it would put half the
8925               	; 	' car on Kelly's BLACK row, where it would read as the Kop. Five pixels
8926               	; 	' across at a fixed centre column tells it from a three-pixel actor dot
8927               	; 	' without needing the height.

     E824 AA16     
10081               	;
10082               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
10083               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
10084               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
10085               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
10086               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
10087               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
10088               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
10089               	; 	'
10090               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
10091               	; 	'
10092               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
10093               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
10094               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
10095               	; calc_bonus:
10096               cvb_CALC_BONUS
10097               	; 	#bonus = 0
10098 E826 04E0  34 	clr @cvb__BONUS
     E828 20D4

10331               	;
10332               	; 	' ======================================================================
10333               	; 	' SOUND
10334               	; 	' ======================================================================
10335               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10336               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10337               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10338               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10339               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
10340               	; sfx_tick:

10688               	;
10689               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10690               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10691               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10692               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10693               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10694               	; 	'
10695               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10696               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10697               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10698               	; 	'
10699               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10700               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10701               	; 	' letter after it.
10702               	;
10703               	; font_bits:
10704               cvb_FONT_BITS
10705               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
10706 ED44 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     ED46 0000     
     ED48 0000     
     ED4A 0000

     EF16 1020     
     EF18 4080     
     EF1A F800     
10823               	;
10824               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
10825               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
10826               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
10827               	; 	#if TI994A
10828               	; 	BANK 1
10829      10E2     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

10836               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10837               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10838               	; 	'
10839               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10840               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10841               	; 	' being small sprites.
10842               	; 	'
10843               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10844               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10845               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10846               	; 	'
10847               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10848               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10849               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10850               	;
10851               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
10852               cvb_SPR_KELLY

11499               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11500               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11501               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11502               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11503               	;
11504               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11505               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11506               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11507               	; 	' unreadable.
11508               	;
11509               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11510               cvb_SCAN_COL3

11591               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11592               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11593               	; 	'
11594               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11595               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11596               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11597               	; 	'
11598               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11599               	; 	' blit is
11600               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11601               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11602               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11603               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11604               	; 	'
11605               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11606               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11607               	; 	'
11608               	; 	' template ids:
11609               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11610               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11611               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11612               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11613               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11614               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11615               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11616               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11617               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11618               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11619               	;
11620               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11621               cvb_STOR_TPL
11622               	; 	DATA BYTE 142,142,142,142,98,142,142,142,142,142,142,98,142,142,142,142
